Theorem 220.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For any navier-stokes module M satisfying the convergence analysis hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 950. The projective resolution has length at most 774 with Betti numbers bounded by 3.

Proof: We proceed by contradiction. Consider the sequence defined by the recursive relation. By the convergence analysis framework applied to navier-stokes structures, the norm ||T^n|| is bounded by 950/774 + ε for arbitrary ε > 0. Taking the limit as ε → 0 yields the desired bound. This completes the proof. QED.

Theorem 220.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let the navier-stokes structure S satisfy the conditions of structural decomposition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 836. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 837 and converges at rate O(1/n^5).

Proof: By the structural decomposition principle applied to navier-stokes theory, we decompose the problem into 836 independent subproblems. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(5/n). QED.

Theorem 220.7 (Homomorphism Theorem)

Statement: Given a navier-stokes algebra A over field F with homomorphism theorem conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 62. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 44/3.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 62 is immediate from the homomorphism theorem property. For the inductive step, we decompose the navier-stokes structure using the homomorphism theorem theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 44. Each subproblem has a solution by the homomorphism theorem fixed-point theorem with constant 3. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
